Use the Value Putting Method: Let A = 60°.

\n

cot((π − A)/2) = cot(90° − 30°) = tan 30° = 1/√3.

\n

tan((2π − A)/2) = tan(180° − 30°) = −tan 30° = −1/√3.

\n

Expression = [2 × (1/√3)] ÷ [1 + (−1/√3)2] = (2/√3) ÷ (4/3) = √3/2.

\n

Since sin 60° = √3/2, the expression simplifies to sinA.

\n

∴ The correct answer is sinA.

\n\n

Alternate Method

\n

Given: [2 cot((π − A)/2)] ÷ [1 + tan2((2π − A)/2)]

\n

Formula Used: cot(π/2 − θ) = tan θ | tan(π − θ) = −tan θ | sin 2θ = (2 tan θ) ÷ (1 + tan2 θ)

\n\n

⇒ Numerator: 2 cot(π/2 − A/2) = 2 tan(A/2)

\n

⇒ Denominator: 1 + tan2(2π/2 − A/2) = 1 + tan2(π − A/2)

\n

⇒ Since tan(π − θ) = −tan θ, then tan2(π − A/2) = (−tan(A/2))2 = tan2(A/2)

\n

⇒ Expression = [2 tan(A/2)] ÷ [1 + tan2(A/2)]

\n

⇒ Applying the sine double angle formula where θ = A/2:

\n

⇒ sin(2 × A/2) = sinA

\n

∴ The correct answer is sinA.

\n\n

Additional Information

\n

Trigonometric Half Angle Formulas

\n

sin θ = (2 tan θ/2) ÷ (1 + tan2 θ/2) and cos θ = (1 − tan2 θ/2) ÷ (1 + tan2 θ/2).

\n

Quadrant Rules (ASTC)

\n

In the 2nd quadrant (π/2 to π), sin and cosec are positive, while tan and cot are negative. cot(π/2 − θ) = tan θ (1st Quadrant) and tan(π − θ) = −tan θ (2nd Quadrant).

\n

\n\n64:Tba7,\nIf tan θ + sec θ = (x - 2)/(x + 2), then what is the value of cos θ?

\n", "options": [{"prompt": "1", "value": "(x<sup>2</sup> - 1)/(x<sup>2</sup> + 1)"}, {"prompt": "2", "value": "(2x<sup>2</sup> - 4)/(2x<sup>2</sup> + 4)"}, {"prompt": "3", "value": "(x<sup>2</sup> - 4)/(x<sup>2</sup> + 4)"}, {"prompt": "4", "value": "(x<sup>2</sup> - 2)/(x<sup>2</sup> + 2)"}], "range": {"start": "", "end": ""}}, "hn": {"value": "यदि tan θ + sec θ = (x - 2)/(x + 2) है, तब cos θ का मान क्या है?", "options": [{"prompt": "1", "value": "(x<sup>2</sup> - 1)/(x<sup>2</sup> + 1)"}, {"prompt": "2", "value": "(2x<sup>2</sup> - 4)/(2x<sup>2</sup> + 4)"}, {"prompt": "3", "value": "(x<sup>2</sup> - 4)/(x<sup>2</sup> + 4)"}, {"prompt": "4", "value": "(x<sup>2</sup> - 2)/(x<sup>2</sup> + 2)"}], "range": {"start": "", "end": ""}}}, "correct_option": "3", "solution_html": {"en": "\n Shortcut Trick\n

If sec θ + tan θ = k, then cos θ = 2k / (k2 + 1).

\n

Here, k = (x − 2) / (x + 2).

\n

cos θ = 2[(x − 2)/(x + 2)] / [((x − 2)/(x + 2))2 + 1]

\n

cos θ = 2(x − 2)(x + 2) / [(x − 2)2 + (x + 2)2]

\n

cos θ = 2(x2 − 4) / (2x2 + 8) = (x2 − 4) / (x2 + 4).

\n

∴ The correct answer is (x2 − 4)/(x2 + 4).

\n\n

Alternate Method

\n

Given: sec θ + tan θ = (x − 2) / (x + 2)

\n

Formula Used: sec2θ − tan2θ = 1 ⇒ (sec θ − tan θ) = 1 / (sec θ + tan θ)

\n\n

Let k = (x − 2) / (x + 2)

\n

⇒ sec θ + tan θ = k

\n

⇒ sec θ − tan θ = 1/k

\n

⇒ 2 sec θ = k + 1/k = (k2 + 1) / k

\n

⇒ sec θ = (k2 + 1) / 2k

\n

⇒ cos θ = 2k / (k2 + 1)

\n

⇒ cos θ = 2[(x − 2)/(x + 2)] / [((x − 2)/(x + 2))2 + 1]

\n

⇒ cos θ = [2(x − 2)(x + 2)] / [(x − 2)2 + (x + 2)2]

\n

⇒ cos θ = 2(x2 − 4) / [x2 − 4x + 4 + x2 + 4x + 4]

\n

⇒ cos θ = 2(x2 − 4) / (2x2 + 8)

\n

⇒ cos θ = (x2 − 4) / (x2 + 4)

\n

∴ The correct answer is (x2 − 4)/(x2 + 4).

\n\n

Additional Information

\n

Trigonometric Identities

\n

The three fundamental identities are sin2θ + cos2θ = 1, 1 + tan2θ = sec2θ, and 1 + cot2θ = cosec2θ.

\n

Secant and Tangent Relation

\n

Since sec2θ − tan2θ = 1, the terms (sec θ + tan θ) and (sec θ − tan θ) are reciprocals of each other.

\n

Pythagorean Triplet in k

\n

For any value k, the set {2k, k2 − 1, k2 + 1} forms a right-angled triangle where k2 + 1 is the hypotenuse.यदि tanθ + secθ = (x - 2)/(x + 2)       ----(1)

\n\n

तब, secθ - tanθ = (x + 2)/(x - 2)        ----(2)

\n\n

समीकरण (1) और (2) को जोड़ने पर,

\n\n

2secθ = (x + 2)/(x - 2) + (x - 2)/(x + 2)

\n\n

⇒ 2secθ = {(x - 2)2 + (x + 2)2}/(x2 - 4)

\n\n

⇒ 2secθ = {2(x2 + 4)}/(x2 - 4)

\n\n

∴ cosθ = (x2 - 4)/(x2 + 4)

(cos 40° - cos 140°)/(sin 80° + sin 20°) का मान क्या है?

\n", "options": [{"prompt": "1", "value": "2&radic;3"}, {"prompt": "2", "value": "2/&radic;3"}, {"prompt": "3", "value": "1/&radic;3"}, {"prompt": "4", "value": "&radic;3"}], "range": {"start": "", "end": ""}}}, "correct_option": "2", "solution_html": {"en": "\n Shortcut Trick\n

Apply Transformation Formulas: Numerator = cos 40° − cos 140° = 2 sin( (40+140)/2 ) sin( (140−40)/2 ) = 2 sin 90° sin 50°.

\n

Denominator = sin 80° + sin 20° = 2 sin( (80+20)/2 ) cos( (80−20)/2 ) = 2 sin 50° cos 30°.

\n

Dividing Numerator by Denominator: (2 × 1 × sin 50°) ÷ (2 × sin 50° × cos 30°) = 1 ÷ cos 30°.

\n

Since cos 30° = √3/2, the result is 1 ÷ (√3/2) = 2/√3.

\n

∴ The correct answer is 2/√3.

\n

Alternate Method

\n

Given: Expression = (cos 40° − cos 140°) ÷ (sin 80° + sin 20°)

\n

Formula Used:

\n

1. cos C − cos D = 2 sin((C+D)/2) sin((D−C)/2)

\n

2. sin A + sin B = 2 sin((A+B)/2) cos((A−B)/2)

\n\n

⇒ Numerator: cos 40° − cos 140° = 2 sin((40+140)/2) sin((140−40)/2)

\n

⇒ Numerator = 2 sin 90° sin 50° = 2 × 1 × sin 50° = 2 sin 50°

\n

⇒ Denominator: sin 80° + sin 20° = 2 sin((80+20)/2) cos((80−20)/2)

\n

⇒ Denominator = 2 sin 50° cos 30°

\n

⇒ Expression = (2 sin 50°) ÷ (2 sin 50° cos 30°)

\n

⇒ Expression = 1 ÷ cos 30°

\n

⇒ Expression = 1 ÷ (√3/2) = 2/√3

\n

∴ The correct answer is 2/√3.

\n

Additional Information

\n

Sum-to-Product Identities

\n

Standard formulas include sin C + sin D = 2 sin((C+D)/2) cos((C−D)/2) and cos C − cos D = 2 sin((C+D)/2) sin((D−C)/2).

\n

Supplementary Angles

\n

cos(180° − θ) = − cos θ. This can be used to write cos 140° as − cos 40°, making the numerator 2 cos 40°.

\n

Complementary Angles

\n

sin θ = cos(90° − θ). For example, sin 50° = cos(90° − 50°) = cos 40°, which simplifies ratios involving both terms.अंश को हल करने पर,

\n\n

⇒ cos 40° - cos140° = - 2 sin[(140° + 40°)/2] × sin[(40° - 140°)/2]

\n\n

(∴ cos A − cos B = − 2 sin(A + B)/2 sin(A−B)/2)

\n\n

⇒ cos 40° - cos140° = 2 sin[(140° + 40°)/2] × sin[(140° - 40°)/2]

\n\n

⇒ cos 40° - cos 140° = 2sin90°.sin50°

\n\n

⇒ cos 40° - cos 140° = 2sin50°

\n\n

हर को हल करने पर,

\n\n

sin 80° + sin 20° = 2 sin[(80° + 20°)/2] × cos[(80° - 20°)/2]

\n\n

(∴ sin A + sin B = 2 sin(A + B)/2 cos(A − B)/2)

\n\n

⇒ sin 80° + sin 20° = 2sin50°.cos30°

\n\n

⇒ sin80° + sin20° = 2sin50° × (√3/2)

\n\n

संबंधित मानों को समीकरण में रखने पर,

\n(cos40° - cos140°)/(sin80° + sin20°) = (2sin50°)/(2sin50° × √3/2) = 2/√3\nb9:Tf47,\n Shortcut Trick\n

Put θ = 60°, then 90° − θ = 30°.

\n

Expression = [1 − tan 30° + sec 30°] ÷ [tan 30° + sec 30° + 1]

\n

⇒ [1 − 1/√3 + 2/√3] ÷ [1/√3 + 2/√3 + 1] = [1 + 1/√3] ÷ [1 + 3/√3]

\n

⇒ [(√3 + 1)/√3] ÷ [(√3 + 3)/√3] = (√3 + 1) / [√3(√3 + 1)] = 1/√3

\n

Checking options for θ = 60°, tan(60°/2) = tan 30° = 1/√3.

\n

∴ The correct answer is tan(θ/2).

\n\n

Alternate Method

\n

Given: [1 − tan(90° − θ) + sec(90° − θ)] ÷ [tan(90° − θ) + sec(90° − θ) + 1]

\n

Formula Used: tan(90° − θ) = cot θ | sec(90° − θ) = csc θ | csc2 θ − cot2 θ = 1

\n\n

⇒ Expression = [1 − cot θ + csc θ] ÷ [cot θ + csc θ + 1]

\n

⇒ Expression = [(csc2 θ − cot2 θ) + (csc θ − cot θ)] ÷ [csc θ + cot θ + 1]

\n

⇒ Expression = [(csc θ − cot θ)(csc θ + cot θ) + (csc θ − cot θ)] ÷ [csc θ + cot θ + 1]

\n

⇒ Expression = [(csc θ − cot θ)(csc θ + cot θ + 1)] ÷ [csc θ + cot θ + 1]

\n

⇒ Expression = csc θ − cot θ

\n

⇒ Expression = 1/sin θ − cos θ/sin θ = (1 − cos θ) / sin θ

\n

⇒ Expression = [2 sin2(θ/2)] ÷ [2 sin(θ/2) cos(θ/2)]

\n

⇒ Expression = sin(θ/2) / cos(θ/2) = tan(θ/2)

\n

∴ The correct answer is tan(θ/2).

\n\n

Additional Information

\n

Complementary Angle Rules

\n

Trigonometric functions change into their co-functions when the angle is (90° − θ): sin ↔ cos, tan ↔ cot, sec ↔ csc.

\n

Pythagorean Identities

\n

Key identities include sin2θ + cos2θ = 1, 1 + tan2θ = sec2θ, and 1 + cot2θ = csc2θ.

\n

Half-Angle Formulas

\n

tan(θ/2) can be expressed as (1 − cos θ)/sin θ or sin θ/(1 + cos θ) or csc θ − cot θ.दिया गया है:

\n\n

[1 - tan(90° - θ) + sec(90° - θ)]/[tan(90° - θ) + sec(90° - θ) + 1]

\n\n

\n
\n⇒ [1 - tan(90° - θ) + sec(90° - θ)]/[tan(90° - θ) + sec(90° - θ) + 1]

\n\n

माना θ = 30° है
\n⇒ [1 - tan60° + sec60°]/[tan60° + sec60° + 1]

\n\n

⇒ (1 - √3 + 2)/(1 + √3 + 2)

\n\n

⇒ {√3(√3 - 1)}/{√3(√3 + 1)}

\n\n

परिमेयकरण करने पर,

\n\n

⇒ (√3 - 1)/(√3 + 1) × (√3 - 1)/(√3 - 1)

\n\n

⇒ (√3 - 1)2/{(√3)2 - 12}

\n\n

⇒ 2 - √3

\n\n

⇒ tan15° = tan(30°/2) = tan(θ/2)

\n\n

∴ [1 - tan(90° - θ) + sec(90° - θ)]/[tan(90° - θ) + sec(90° - θ) + 1] का मान tan(θ/2) है।

\n

\n

ASTC Rule (Quadrants)

\n

In Trigonometry, sin is positive in Q1 and Q2, while cos is positive only in Q1 and Q4. This determines the sign when converting (180° − θ).

Additional Information

\n

Pythagoras Theorem

\n

In any right-angled triangle, the square of the hypotenuse is equal to the sum of the squares of the other two sides: a2 + b2 = c2.

\n

Standard triplets like (3, 4, 5), (5, 12, 13), and (8, 15, 17) often appear in exams. Multiples of these, such as (20, 48, 52), also satisfy the theorem.

Final mixture volume = 4 + 1 = 5 litres.

\n

Final ratio is 1 : 1 → Fruit juice = 2.5 litres and Water = 2.5 litres.

\n

Since only water was added, the amount of juice remains the same.

\n

In the original 4 litres: Juice = 2.5 litres, Water = 4 − 2.5 = 1.5 litres.

\n

Initial Ratio Juice : Water = 2.5 : 1.5 = 25 : 15 = 5 : 3.

\n

Comparing with 5 : x → x = 3.

\n

∴ The correct answer is 3.

\n

Alternate Method

\n

Given:

\n

Initial blend ratio (Juice : Water) = 5 : x

\n

Initial volume of blend = 4 litres

\n

Water added = 1 litre

\n

Final ratio (Juice : Water) = 1 : 1

\n\n

Calculations:

\n

⇒ Total volume of final mixture = 4 + 1 = 5 litres

\n

⇒ Final ratio is 1 : 1, so Juice = Water = 5 ÷ 2 = 2.5 litres

\n

⇒ In the initial 4 litres, amount of fruit juice = 2.5 litres

\n

⇒ In the initial 4 litres, amount of water = 4 − 2.5 = 1.5 litres

\n

⇒ Initial Ratio (Juice : Water) = 2.5 : 1.5

\n

⇒ 5 : x = 2.5 : 1.5

\n

⇒ x = (5 × 1.5) ÷ 2.5

\n

⇒ x = 7.5 ÷ 2.5 = 3

\n

∴ The correct answer is 3.

\n

Additional Information

\n

Ratio of Components

\n

If a mixture of volume V has components A and B in ratio a : b, then quantity of A = V × [a / (a + b)] and quantity of B = V × [b / (a + b)].

\n

Constant Quantity Principle

\n

When only one component of a mixture is added or removed, the absolute quantity of the other component remains unchanged.

\n

Dilution Formula

\n

Concentration C1V1 = C2V2, where C is the concentration of the non-added component and V is the total volume.दिया हुआ:

Alternate Method

\n\n

⇒ New quantity of Copper = 3x + 250

\n

⇒ New quantity of Tin = 2x

\n

⇒ (3x + 250) ÷ 2x = 2 ÷ 1

⇒ 4x − 3x = 250

\n

⇒ x = 250

\n

⇒ Amount of tin in the alloy = 2x = 2 × 250

\n

⇒ Amount of tin = 500 gm

\n

∴ The correct answer is 500 gm.

Additional Information

\n

Constant Quantity Rule

\n

In problems where only one component of a mixture is added or removed, the quantity of the other component remains constant. Equating its ratio units simplifies calculations significantly.

\n

Ratio Balancing

\n

To compare two ratios when one part remains unchanged, multiply the terms of the ratios by suitable numbers so that the units of the constant part become equal in both ratios.

\n

Alligation Rule

\n

This method is used to find the ratio in which two ingredients at given prices or concentrations must be mixed to produce a mixture of a desired price or concentration.∵ मिश्र धातु में ताम्बा और टिन 3:2 के अनुपात में है

B share = 5 units = 5 × 25,000 = ₹1,25,000.

\n

∴ The correct answer is ₹1,25,000.

\n\n

Alternate Method

\n

Given:

\n

Investment of A = ₹5 Lakhs, Time = 12 months

\n

Investment of B = ₹10 Lakhs, Time = 12 − 2 = 10 months

\n

Investment of C = ₹20 Lakhs, Time = 10 − 4 = 6 months

\n

Total Profit = ₹3,50,000

\n

Formula:

\n

Profit Share Ratio = (C1 × T1) : (C2 × T2) : (C3 × T3)

\n\n

Calculations:

\n

⇒ Profit Ratio = (5 × 12) : (10 × 10) : (20 × 6)

\n

⇒ Profit Ratio = 60 : 100 : 120

\n

⇒ Simplifying by dividing by 20: 3 : 5 : 6

\n

⇒ Sum of ratio parts = 3 + 5 + 6 = 14

\n

⇒ B share in profit = (5 ÷ 14) × 3,50,000

\n

⇒ B share = 5 × 25,000

\n

⇒ B share = ₹1,25,000

\n

∴ The correct answer is ₹1,25,000.

\n\n

Additional Information

\n

Basic Partnership Rule

\n

Profit of a partner is directly proportional to the product of their Investment and the Time period of investment (P ∝ I × T).

\n

Types of Partners

\n

Active partners manage the business and may receive a salary, while Sleeping partners only invest capital and share profit based on the investment ratio.

\n

Compound Partnership

\n

In a compound partnership, the capitals are invested for different periods of time. Ratio of profits = (C1T1) : (C2T2) : ... : (CnTn).

Distributed Profit = 50,000 ÷ 2 = ₹ 25,000.

\n

Adjusted Ratio (A : B : C) = 3 : (6 × 3/4) : 5 = 3 : 4.5 : 5.

\n

Total units = 3 + 4.5 + 5 = 12.5 units.

\n

12.5 units = 25,000 ⇒ 1 unit = 2,000.

\n

\n\n

Alternate Method

\n

Given:

\n

Total Profit = ₹ 50,000

\n

Investment Ratio (A : B : C) = 3 : 6 : 5

\n

Sleeping partner condition: B gets 3/4th of his share.

\n

Formula Used:

\n

Profit Share = Capital × Time (adjusted for partner type)

\n\n

Calculations:

\n

⇒ Reinvested amount = 50,000 ÷ 2 = ₹ 25,000

\n

⇒ Distributed profit = 50,000 − 25,000 = ₹ 25,000

\n

⇒ Effective share of A = 3 units

\n

⇒ Effective share of B = 6 × (3/4) = 4.5 units

\n

⇒ Effective share of C = 5 units

\n

⇒ Ratio of Profit (A : B : C) = 3 : 4.5 : 5

\n

⇒ To simplify the ratio, multiply by 2: (3 × 2) : (4.5 × 2) : (5 × 2) = 6 : 9 : 10

\n

⇒ Sum of ratio = 6 + 9 + 10 = 25 parts

\n

⇒ C's Share = (10 ÷ 25) × 25,000

\n

⇒ C's Share = 10 × 1,000 = ₹ 10,000

\n

∴ The correct answer is ₹10000.

\n\n

Additional Information

\n

Working vs Sleeping Partner

\n

A working partner manages the business and may receive an extra salary or higher share, while a sleeping partner only invests money and receives a lower profit share.

\n

Partnership Profit Ratio

\n

Profit Share Ratio = (Capital1 × Time1) : (Capital2 × Time2). If time is constant, profit is shared in the ratio of investments.

\n

Distributed Profit

\n

Distributed Profit = Total Profit − Retained Earnings (or Reinvestment). Only the distributed portion is divided among partners using the profit-sharing ratio.

Total work (LCM of 55, 66, and 12) = 660 units.

\n

Combined efficiency of A, B, and C = 660 ÷ 12 = 55 units/day.

\n

Individual efficiencies: A = 660 ÷ 55 = 12 units/day; B = 660 ÷ 66 = 10 units/day.

\n

Efficiency of C = 55 − (12 + 10) = 33 units/day.

\n

Time taken by C alone = 660 ÷ 33 = 20 days.

\n

∴ The correct answer is 20 days.

Alternate Method

\n

Given:

\n

Time taken by A = 55 days

\n

Time taken by B = 66 days

\n

Time taken by (A + B + C) = 12 days

\n

Formula Used: Total Work = Efficiency × Time

\n\n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n \n
PersonDaysTotal WorkEfficiency
A5566012
B6610
A + B + C1255
\n

Calculations:

\n

⇒ Efficiency of A = 660 ÷ 55 = 12

\n

⇒ Efficiency of B = 660 ÷ 66 = 10

\n

⇒ Efficiency of (A + B + C) = 660 ÷ 12 = 55

\n

⇒ Efficiency of C = Eff(A+B+C) − Eff(A) − Eff(B)

\n

⇒ Efficiency of C = 55 − 12 − 10 = 33

\n

⇒ Time taken by C = Total Work ÷ Efficiency of C

\n

⇒ Time = 660 ÷ 33 = 20 days

\n

∴ The correct answer is 20 days.

Additional Information

\n

Work and Efficiency Relationship

\n

Efficiency is inversely proportional to the time taken to complete the work. Efficiency = Total Work ÷ Time.

\n

Combined Work Formula

\n

If three people A, B, and C can complete a work in a, b, and c days respectively, their combined efficiency is 1/a + 1/b + 1/c.

\n

Pipes and Cisterns

\n

This concept is identical to Time and Work, where an inlet pipe has positive efficiency and an outlet pipe (leak) has negative efficiency.∵ A और B एक कार्य को करने में क्रमशः 55 और 66 दिनों का समय लेते हैं।

\nca:Td52,\n Shortcut Trick\n

Total work = LCM(12, 36) = 36 units.

\n

Combined efficiency (A+B+C) = 36 ÷ 12 = 3 units/day.

\n

Efficiency of C = 36 ÷ 36 = 1 unit/day.

\n

Efficiency of (A+B) = Efficiency(A+B+C) − Efficiency(C) = 3 − 1 = 2 units/day.

\n

Required time for A and B = 36 ÷ 2 = 18 days.

\n

∴ The correct answer is 18 days.

\n

Additional Information

\n

Inverse Relation of Time and Efficiency

\n

Efficiency is inversely proportional to the time taken to complete a work. If the ratio of time is x:y, then the ratio of efficiency is y:x.

\n

Combined Work Formula

\n

If two people can do a work in 'a' and 'b' days respectively, then together they can finish it in (a × b) ÷ (a + b) days.

\n

Work and Wages

\n

Wages are always distributed in the ratio of the work done by individuals, which is also the ratio of their efficiencies if they work for the same amount of time.दिया हुआ:

⇒ Time taken by (A + B) = 18 ÷ 3 = 6 days

\n

∴ The correct answer is 6 days.

\n\n

Additional Information

\n

Total Work Formula

\n

Total Work = Time taken × Efficiency. For multiple people, Total Efficiency = Σ(Individual Efficiencies).

\n

Pipes and Cisterns

\n

Similar to time and work, where inlet pipes have positive efficiency and outlet pipes have negative efficiency.

\n

Work and Wages

\n

Wages are distributed in the ratio of the work done, which is usually the ratio of their respective efficiencies.

\n

LCM Method

\n

Total work is assumed as the LCM of the number of days to avoid calculations in fractions.गणना:

\n\n

माना A कार्य को ‘x’ दिनों में और B कार्य को ‘y’ दिनों में पूर्ण कर सकता है

\n

Successive Discounts

\n

If two successive discounts of a% and b% are given, the equivalent single discount is given by [a + b − (a × b)/100]%.

Mark-up Percentage

\n

\n

Relation between CP and MP

\n

CP ÷ MP = (100 − Discount %) ÷ (100 + Profit %). This is a very useful ratio for solving complex Profit & Loss problems.

Marked Price (MP) = CP + (Markup% of CP)

\n

Selling Price (SP) = MP − (Discount% of MP)

\n\n

Calculations:

\n

⇒ Marked Price (MP) = 750 + (120/100) × 750

\n

⇒ MP = 750 + 900 = ₹1650

\n

⇒ Selling Price (SP) = 1650 − (30/100) × 1650

\n

⇒ SP = 1650 − 495

\n

⇒ SP = ₹1155

\n

∴ The correct answer is ₹1155.

\n

Additional Information

\n

Effective Profit Percentage

\n

Effective Profit% = M − D − (M × D)/100, where M is markup% and D is discount%.

\n

Relationship between CP and MP

\n

MP/CP = (100 + Profit%) / (100 − Discount%). This is used to find markup directly when Profit and Discount are known.

\n

Successive Discounts

\n

Two successive discounts d1% and d2% are equivalent to a single discount of [d1 + d2 − (d1 × d2)/100]%.

\n

\n

If a total amount T is divided in the ratio x : y, the parts are [x / (x + y)] × T and [y / (x + y)] × T.

\n

The inverse ratio of a : b is 1/a : 1/b, which simplifies to b : a.

\n

\n

\n

If a : b = c : d, then b : a = d : c. This property is useful when dealing with reciprocal proportions in work or speed problems.

\n

If one part of the ratio remains unchanged, multiply the ratios by factors that make the constant part equal in both the initial and final states.

Basic Average Formula

\n

Assume base average = ₹1250

\n

\n

Distribute surplus among all 5 shirts = 400 ÷ 5 = ₹80

\n

Final Average = 1250 + 80 = ₹1330

\n

∴ The correct answer is ₹1330.

\n

Alternate Method

\n

\n

Formula Used: Combined Average = (Total Value) ÷ (Total Number of items)

\n\n

⇒ Total price of 3 shirts = 3 × 1250 = ₹3750

\n

⇒ Total price of 2 shirts = 2 × 1450 = ₹2900

\n

⇒ Total sum of all 5 shirts = 3750 + 2900 = ₹6650

\n

⇒ Average price of 5 shirts = 6650 ÷ 5 = ₹1330

\n

∴ The correct answer is ₹1330.

Additional Information

\n

Weighted Average Formula

\n

When two groups with different averages are combined, the new average is Aavg = (n1A1 + n2A2) ÷ (n1 + n2).

\n

Deviation Method for Averages

\n

It simplifies calculations by picking an assumed average and calculating the net surplus or deficit relative to it.

\n

Average of Averages

\n

The simple average of two averages (A1 + A2) ÷ 2 only works if the number of items in both groups is equal (n1 = n2).

\n

\n

⇒ Runs required by Team B in last 10 overs = 10 × 6.5

\n

\n

\n

⇒ Current score = 305 − 65 = 240

\n

∴ The correct answer is 240.

Run Rate Formula

\n

Run Rate = (Total runs scored) ÷ (Total overs bowled). It represents the average runs per over.

\n

Required Run Rate

\n

Required Run Rate = (Remaining runs needed) ÷ (Remaining overs). Used to calculate pace needed to win or tie.

\n∴ 40 ਓਵਰਾਂ ਤੱਕ ਟੀਮ B ਦਾ ਸਕੋਰ = 305 - 65 = 240", "sd": "", "ur": "", "sat": "", "mai": "", "brx": "", "doi": "", "sa": "", "grt": "", "kha": "", "lus": "", "bo": "", "trp": ""}, "tags": ["Average"], "concept": "Average", "confidence": "high"}, {"qid": "5c2c6bf0fdb8bb3444ee6028", "qs_no": 84, "pos_marks": 2.0, "neg_marks": 0.5, "type": "mcq", "langs": {"en": {"value": "Average of all even numbers between 222 and 250 is _________.", "options": [{"prompt": "1", "value": "234"}, {"prompt": "2", "value": "232"}, {"prompt": "3", "value": "236"}, {"prompt": "4", "value": "230"}], "range": {"start": "", "end": ""}}, "hn": {"value": "222 और 250 के बीच सभी सम संख्याओं का औसत ________ है।", "options": [{"prompt": "1", "value": "234"}, {"prompt": "2", "value": "232"}, {"prompt": "3", "value": "236"}, {"prompt": "4", "value": "230"}], "range": {"start": "", "end": ""}}}, "correct_option": "3", "solution_html": {"en": "\n Shortcut Trick\n

For any sequence in Arithmetic Progression (AP), the Average is the mean of the first and last terms.

\n

First even number after 222 = 224

\n

Last even number before 250 = 248

\n

Shortcut: Average = (First Term + Last Term) ÷ 2

\n

Average = (224 + 248) ÷ 2 = 472 ÷ 2 = 236

\n

∴ The correct answer is 236.

\n\n

Alternate Method

\n

Given: Even numbers between 222 and 250.

\n

Formula Used: Average of an Arithmetic Progression = (a + l) ÷ 2, where 'a' is the first term and 'l' is the last term.

\n\n

The even numbers between 222 and 250 are: 224, 226, 228, 230, 232, 234, 236, 238, 240, 242, 244, 246, 248.

\n

⇒ First term (a) = 224

\n

⇒ Last term (l) = 248

\n

⇒ Common difference (d) = 2

\n

⇒ Number of terms (n) = [(Last − First) ÷ d] + 1

\n

⇒ n = [(248 − 224) ÷ 2] + 1 = (24 ÷ 2) + 1 = 12 + 1 = 13

\n

⇒ Sum of terms = (n ÷ 2) × (a + l)

\n

⇒ Sum = (13 ÷ 2) × (224 + 248) = 6.5 × 472 = 3068

\n

⇒ Average = Sum ÷ n = 3068 ÷ 13

\n

⇒ Average = 236

\n

∴ The correct answer is 236.

\n\n

Additional Information

\n

Average of Consecutive Numbers

\n

For any set of consecutive integers, even numbers, or odd numbers, the average is always the middle term if the number of terms is odd, or the mean of the two middle terms if even.

\n

Sum of Even Numbers

\n

The sum of the first n even numbers is given by the formula: Sum = n(n + 1). The average of the first n even numbers is simply (n + 1).

\n

Inclusive vs Exclusive Range

\n

Between x and y usually implies excluding x and y. From x to y implies including both x and y. In this specific symmetric case, the average remains the same.∵ 222 और 250 के बीच की संख्या भी 224, 226, 228,… .248 है

\n\n

श्रृंखला का अंतिम तत्व (an) = पहला तत्व (a) + [तत्वों की संख्या (n) - 1] × सिलसिलेवार तत्वों के बीच अंतर (d)

\n\n

an = a + (n - 1)d

\n\n

⇒ 248 = 224 + (n - 1) × 2

\n\n

⇒ n = 13

\n\n

श्रृंखला का योग (Sn) = (n/2) (a + an)

\n\n

⇒ श्रृंखला का योग (Sn) = (13/2) × (224 + 248)

\n\n

⇒ श्रृंखला का योग (Sn) = 13 × 236

\n\n

222 और 250 के बीच सभी सम संख्याओं का औसत = Sn/n

\n\n

⇒ 222 और 250 के बीच सभी सम संख्याओं का औसत = (13 × 236)/13

\n

\n

\n

\n

Alternate Method

Additional Information

\n

Marked Price and Discount

\n

Marked Price (MP) is the listed price. Selling Price (SP) = MP − Discount. Discount % = (Discount ÷ MP) × 100.

\n

Dishonest Dealer Concept

\n

Profit % = (Error ÷ True Value − Error) × 100, used when a dealer uses false weights for profit.

\n

Successive Percentage Change

\n

Total change for two successive increases/decreases a% and b% is given by [a + b + (ab ÷ 100)]%.

Additional Information

\n

Effective Profit Percentage

\n

When two profits x% and y% are applied successively, the net profit percentage is given by [x + y + (xy/100)]%.

\n

Cost Price from Selling Price

\n

If an item is sold at a profit of P%, then the Cost Price (CP) = [Selling Price (SP) × 100] ÷ (100 + P).

\n

Successive Discounts

\n

Similar to successive profits, for successive discounts of d1% and d2%, the single equivalent discount is [d1 + d2 − (d1d2/100)]%.

\n

\n

\n

\n\n

\n

Alligation Rule

\n

It is a rule that enables us to find the ratio in which two or more ingredients at their respective prices must be mixed to produce a mixture of a desired price.

Difference between two Selling Prices = 58,000 − 46,000 = ₹12,000.

\n

Given: Profit = 2 × Loss ⇒ Ratio of Loss : Profit = 1 : 2.

\n

Total units for the gap = 1 + 2 = 3 units.

\n

3 units = ₹12,000 ⇒ 1 unit (Loss) = ₹4,000.

\n

Cost Price = Selling Price1 + Loss = 46,000 + 4,000 = ₹50,000.

\n

∴ The correct answer is ₹50000.

\n

Alternate Method

\n

Given:

\n

Selling Price 1 (SP1) = ₹46,000

\n

Selling Price 2 (SP2) = ₹58,000

\n

Let the Loss be x. Then Profit = 2x.

\n\n

Calculations:

\n

We know that: Cost Price (CP) = SP1 + Loss = SP2 − Profit

\n

⇒ 46,000 + x = 58,000 − 2x

\n

⇒ x + 2x = 58,000 − 46,000

\n

⇒ 3x = 12,000

\n

⇒ x = 12,000 ÷ 3 = 4,000

\n

⇒ CP = 46,000 + 4,000

\n

⇒ CP = 50,000

\n

∴ The correct answer is ₹50000.

\n

Additional Information

\n

Basic Profit and Loss

\n

Profit occurs when SP > CP (Profit = SP − CP), and Loss occurs when CP > SP (Loss = CP − SP).

\n

Profit and Loss Percentage

\n

Profit% = (Profit ÷ CP) × 100 and Loss% = (Loss ÷ CP) × 100. Both are always calculated on the Cost Price unless specified otherwise.

\n

Direct Formula for Constant CP

\n

If Profit at SP2 is 'n' times the Loss at SP1, then CP = (n × SP1 + SP2) ÷ (n + 1).

Shortcut Trick\n

Let initial expenditure be 45 (proportional to old price).

\n

Allowed expenditure after 5% increase = 45 × 1.05 = 47.25.

\n

Expected expenditure at new price without reduction = 50.

\n

Required reduction in consumption (in terms of price) = 50 − 47.25 = 2.75.

\n

% Reduction = (2.75 ÷ 50) × 100 = 5.5%.

\n

∴ The correct answer is 5.5%.

\n\n

Alternate Method

\n\n

Given:

\n

Old price = Rs. 45/litre

\n

New price = Rs. 50/litre

\n

Allowed increase in expenditure = 5%

\n\n

Formula Used:

\n

Expenditure = Price × Consumption

\n

% Reduction = [(Old Value − New Value) ÷ Old Value] × 100

\n\n\n\n

Calculation:

\n

⇒ Let the initial consumption be 100 litres.

\n

⇒ Initial Expenditure = 45 × 100 = 4500 Rs.

\n

⇒ Allowed New Expenditure = 4500 + (5% of 4500)

\n

⇒ Allowed New Expenditure = 4500 + 225 = 4725 Rs.

\n

⇒ New Consumption = Allowed New Expenditure ÷ New Price

\n

⇒ New Consumption = 4725 ÷ 50 = 94.5 litres.

\n

⇒ Reduction in consumption = 100 − 94.5 = 5.5 litres.

\n

⇒ % Reduction = (5.5 ÷ 100) × 100 = 5.5%.

\n

∴ The correct answer is 5.5%.

\n\n

Additional Information

\n\n

Price, Consumption & Expenditure

\n

Expenditure = Price × Consumption. If expenditure is kept constant, consumption is inversely proportional to price (C1 ÷ C2 = P2 ÷ P1).

\n\n

Constant Expenditure Trick

\n

If the price of a commodity increases by R%, the percentage reduction in consumption to keep expenditure the same is [R ÷ (100 + R)] × 100%.

\n\n

Successive Percentage Change

\n

When a value undergoes two successive changes of A% and B%, the net percentage change is given by (A + B + AB ÷ 100)%.

\n\n

Base Change Concept

\n

If value A is x% more than B, then B is [x ÷ (100 + x)] × 100% less than A. This logic directly maps to price and consumption ratio problems.

Additional Information

\n

Unit Conversion Rules

\n

To convert speed from km/h to m/s, multiply by 5/18. To convert from m/s to km/h, multiply by 18/5.

\n

Average Speed for Two Parts

\n

If a journey is divided into two equal distances with speeds v1 and v2, the average speed = (2 × v1 × v2) ÷ (v1 + v2).

\n

When the distance traveled is the same for both parts of the journey, use the harmonic mean formula.

\n

Average Speed = (2 × x × y) ÷ (x + y)

\n

Where x = 24 km/hr and y = 40 km/hr.

\n

Average Speed = (2 × 24 × 40) ÷ (24 + 40) = 1920 ÷ 64 = 30 km/hr.

\n

∴ The correct answer is 30 km/hr.

\n\n

Alternate Method

\n

Given:

\n

Upstream Speed (u) = 24 km/hr

\n

Downstream Speed (v) = 40 km/hr

\n

Formula: Average Speed = Total Distance ÷ Total Time

\n\n

⇒ Let the distance between the two points be the LCM of 24 and 40.

\n

⇒ LCM(24, 40) = 120 km.

\n

⇒ Time taken while going upstream (t1) = 120 ÷ 24 = 5 hours.

\n

⇒ Time taken while coming back downstream (t2) = 120 ÷ 40 = 3 hours.

\n

⇒ Total Distance = 120 + 120 = 240 km.

\n

⇒ Total Time = 5 + 3 = 8 hours.

\n

⇒ Average Speed = 240 ÷ 8 = 30 km/hr.

\n

∴ The correct answer is 30 km/hr.

\n\n

Additional Information

\n

Speed of Boat in Still Water

\n

It is the average of downstream and upstream speeds: Speedboat = (Downstream + Upstream) ÷ 2.

\n

Speed of the Stream (Current)

\n

It is half the difference between downstream and upstream speeds: Speedstream = (Downstream − Upstream) ÷ 2.

\n

General Average Speed

\n

For unequal distances, Average Speed = (d1 + d2 + ... + dn) ÷ (t1 + t2 + ... + tn).औसत गति = {2 × गति (धारा के प्रतिकूल) × गति (धारा के अनुकूल)} / {गति (प्रतिकूल) + गति (अनुकूल)}

\n\n

⇒ औसत गति = (2 × 24 × 40) / (24 + 40) = 30

When two objects move towards each other, the Relative Speed is the sum of their individual speeds.

\n

Relative Speed = 75 + 60 = 135 km/hr

\n

Time given = 20 minutes = 20 ÷ 60 = 1/3 hour

\n

Initial Distance = Relative Speed × Time = 135 × (1/3) = 45 km

\n

∴ The correct answer is 45 km.

\n

Alternate Method

\n

Given:

\n

Speed of biker A (SA) = 75 km/hr

\n

Speed of biker B (SB) = 60 km/hr

\n

Time (T) = 20 minutes

\n

Formula Used:

\n

Distance = Relative Speed × Time

\n

Relative Speed (Opposite Direction) = S1 + S2

\n\n

Calculations:

\n

⇒ Convert time into hours: Time = 20 ÷ 60 = 1/3 hour

\n

⇒ Relative Speed = SA + SB

\n

⇒ Relative Speed = 75 + 60 = 135 km/hr

\n

⇒ Total Distance = 135 × (1/3)

\n

⇒ Total Distance = 45 km

\n

∴ The correct answer is 45 km.

\n

Additional Information

\n

Relative Speed in Same Direction

\n

When two objects move in the same direction, Relative Speed = |S1 − S2|.

\n

Speed Unit Conversion

\n

To convert km/hr to m/s, multiply the speed by 5/18. To convert m/s to km/hr, multiply by 18/5.

Key Points
\n
    \n
  • The speed of the bus excluding stoppages is 80 kmph. This implies that in one hour of continuous running, the bus covers 80 kilometers.
    • \n
  • The speed of the bus including stoppages is 60 kmph. This means that in one actual hour (including stop time), the bus covers only 60 kilometers.
    • \n
  • The reduction in distance covered in one hour due to stops is: 80 − 60 = 20 km.
    • \n
  • The stop time is equivalent to the time the bus would have taken to cover those 20 km if it had been running at its non-stop speed of 80 kmph.
    • \n
  • Using the formula: Time = Distance ÷ Speed.
    • \n
  • Stoppage time in hours = 20 ÷ 80 = 1/4 hour.
    • \n
  • To convert hours into minutes: 1/4 × 60 = 15 minutes.
    • \n
  • Therefore, the bus stops for 15 minutes every hour.
    • \n
\n
Additional Information
\n
    \n
  • A quick shortcut formula for this type of problem is: Stop time per hour = (Faster Speed − Slower Speed) ÷ Faster Speed.
    • \n
  • Applying the formula: (80 − 60) ÷ 80 = 20 ÷ 80 = 1/4 hour. Multiply by 60 to get 15 minutes.
    • \n
  • This logic is based on the fact that the deficit in distance is entirely due to the time spent at stoppages, during which the bus is not moving at its constant speed.

    ଏକ ଘଣ୍ଟାରେ 80 କିଲୋମିଟର ପ୍ରତି ଘଣ୍ଟାରେ ବିନା ଷ୍ଟପେଜରେ ବସ୍ ଦ୍ୱାରା ଅତିକ୍ରମ ଦୂରତା = 80 km

    ଗୋଟିଏ ଘଣ୍ଟାରେ 60 କିଲୋମିଟର ପ୍ରତି ଘଣ୍ଟାରେ ଅଟକିବା ସହିତ ବସ୍ ଦ୍ୱାରା ଅତିକ୍ରମ ଦୂରତା = 60 km

    \n\n

    ଘଣ୍ଟା ପ୍ରତି ବନ୍ଦ ପାଇଁ ସମୟ = (80 କିଲୋମିଟର - 60 କିଲୋମିଟର) / 80 କିଲୋମିଟର / ଘଣ୍ଟା 

    \n\n

    ⇒ ପ୍ରତି ଘଣ୍ଟା ବନ୍ଦ ପାଇଁ ସମୟ = 1/4 ଘଣ୍ଟା 

    \n\n

    CI on Consecutive Years

    \n

    In compound interest, the amount at the end of year n becomes the principal for year n+1. Similarly, the interest for year n+1 is the interest on the amount at the end of year n.

    \n

    Effective Rate of Interest

    \n

    For a rate r% compounded annually for 2 years, the effective rate is [r + r + (r × r)/100]%.

    \n

    Difference between SI and CI

    \n

    For 2 years, the difference D = P × (r/100)2. For the 1st year, SI and CI are equal if compounded annually.

    Additional Information

    \n

    Compound Interest with Successive Rates

    \n

    When interest rates vary each year, the final amount is calculated as A = P × (1 + r1/100) × (1 + r2/100) × ... × (1 + rn/100).

    \n

    Effective Percentage Rate

    \n

    For two successive rates r1 and r2, the net increase is given by [r1 + r2 + (r1 × r2)/100] %.

    \n

    Fractional Method

    \n

    Convert percentages to fractions (e.g., 8% = 2/25, 9% = 9/100) and multiply ratios: P × (27/25) × (109/100) = A.

    \n

    For semi-annual compounding, rate = 20% ÷ 2 = 10% and periods = 2.

    \n

    \n

    Additional Information

    \n

    Effective Interest Rate Formula

    \n

    For two successive interest periods with rates x% and y%, the net effective rate is calculated as x + y + (x × y / 100)%.

    \n

    Compounding Frequency Rules

    \n

    If interest is compounded 'k' times a year, the periodic rate becomes R/k and the number of periods becomes T × k.

    Difference for 2 years (Shortcut)

    \n

    The difference between CI and SI for 2 years (or 2 periods) is given by P × (R/100)2.
